[109] User:
VSM Formal Notation — Language Specification
 Version: 0.3
 Target: v1.0 (annotated, current) → v2.0 (pure notation)
 Authors: VSF Project
 Status: DRAFT
 Changelog: 0.2 — L-relativity, D4 refined, D10/D11/D11b/D12 new,
 tick(L), reframe(), @status, protocol layer (Part IX)
 0.3 — A9/A9b/A9c/A9d (budget exhaustion + degradation modes),
 A10 (escalation target determinism), A11 (S4 activation gate),
 D11b trust formula: quantitative weights + reachability component
 (validated against vsl_core.v1.2, 2026-04-09)
 ---
 Preamble
 This document defines the formal notation used by the Viable System Framework (VSF)
 to encode systems, rules, memory, and inter-system communication.
 The notation has two audiences:
 Humans: read the annotated form (notation + `// comment`)
 Machines (LLMs, parsers): read the pure notation form (no comments required)
 Version declaration is mandatory in every `.vsm` file:
     @vsm 0.2          // every machine or human must verify this first
                       // Human: every .vsm file must declare its spec version
 ---
 Part I — Alphabet and Symbols
 1.1 Primitive symbols
 Symbol	Name	Human meaning
 `V`	Viable System constructor	"a system that can maintain its identity"
 `S1`..`S5`	Subsystem selectors	Beer's five management systems
 `E`	Environment	observable context at level L
 `L`	Level parameter	recursion depth — frame-local, not absolute (see §2 note)
 `B`	Budget	total available resource at level L
 `φ`	Channel operator	communication between levels
 `↑`	Upward direction	child → parent (attenuation)
 `↓`	Downward direction	parent → child (amplification)
 `ψ`	Structural coupling	horizontal S4↔S4 channel between non-sibling VSMs
 `θ`	Parameter set	configuration passed downward
 `τ`	Threshold	local resolution capacity
 `σ`	Significance threshold	multi-dimensional escalation threshold
 `r`	Resolution rate	fraction of signals resolved locally
 `∅`	Empty resolution	signal could not be resolved locally
 `∘`	Composition	"A must go through B"
 `∀`	Universal quantifier	"for every"
 `∃`	Existential quantifier	"there exists at least one"
 `⊕`	Variety combination	regulatory variety (Ashby)
 `≤`	Variety bound	Ashby's requisite variety condition
 `⟹`	Escalation	signal propagates to parent level
 `≡`	Equivalence	structural identity between systems
 `⊥`	Infeasible	system cannot maintain viability
 `@`	Decorator	metadata attached to a term
 `#`	Alias	short name for a longer expression
 1.2 Lifecycle annotations
     @status ∈ { planned, onboarding, iterating, testing, production, deprecated }
     // Human: attached to V() or unit() declarations.
     // Extends D5 (recursion condition) with observable lifecycle state.
     // Example: V("unit-x", L=1) @status=production
 1.3 File types
 Extension	Content	Audience
 `.vsm`	Pure VSM notation	Machines + advanced humans
 `.vsm.md`	Annotated VSM (notation + prose comments)	Humans during development
 `events.jsonl`	Upward channel payload	Runtime
 `params.json`	Downward channel payload	Runtime
 ---
 Part II — Axioms
 Axioms are the non-derivable truths of the system.
 They cannot be proven from within the framework — they are the foundation.
 Note on L (level parameter)
 L is frame-local, not absolute.
     L=0  = current root of analysis — changes when context changes
     V.id = stable identifier of the system — never changes (ChatGPT M3)
     L    = view parameter of the current analysis session
     // V.id immutability (M3 — formally constrained):
     rule(V.id): immutable ∧ globally_unique
     // V.id must never be renamed, reassigned, or aliased.
     // Stability of V.id is what makes reframe() safe — the frame changes, the system does not.
     // Human: "tool-conductor" is not inherently L=0.
     // It is L=0 when analyzed in isolation.
     // It becomes L=1 when a governing organization is added above it.
     // The system itself does not change — the frame of analysis changes.
     // Channel schemas reference V.id; L is used only for local reasoning.
     // Recursion depth is not known at design time — discovered at runtime.
 A1 — Recursion axiom
     S1(L) = { V_i(L+1) }_{i=1}^{n}
     // Human: every operational unit of S1 at level L is itself a complete
     // viable system at level L+1. This is what makes the model scale-invariant.
     // You cannot have a "partial" viable system — it either is one, or it isn't.
 A2 — Budget monotonicity axiom
     B(child) ≤ B(parent)    ∀ (parent, child) ∈ V.hierarchy
     // Human: a child system never has more resources than its parent.
     // Expressed as a structural relation (parent-child), not an index relation.
     // L is frame-local — the invariant holds regardless of how levels are
     // numbered in the current analysis frame.
     // Equivalent to B(L+1) ≤ B(L) within a fixed frame.
 A3 — Physical resource axiom
     B(L) = min( B_tokens(L), B_compute(L), B_time(L) )
     // Human: the effective budget is the most constrained dimension.
     // Token budget and thermal budget are the same *type* of thing —
     // finite physical resources that the system must account for.
     // Neither can be borrowed from the future.
 A4 — Channel axiom (no direct coupling)
     ∀ V_i(L+1), V_j(L+1) where i ≠ j:
       V_i ↛ V_j    // no direct channel between siblings
       V_i → φ↑ → S3(L) → φ↓ → V_j    // all sibling communication via parent S3
     // Human: siblings cannot talk directly. All communication goes through
     // the parent's S3. This prevents hidden dependencies the parent cannot observe.
     // Note: ψ (D11) is NOT a sibling channel — it connects different VSM trees.
     // A4 applies within a tree; ψ connects trees.
 A5 — Autonomy ceiling axiom
     ∀ op ∈ S1(L):
       op.autonomy ≤ S5(L).ceiling
     // Corollary 1 — ceiling monotonicity:
     ∀ L > 0: S5(L).ceiling ≤ S5(L-1).ceiling
     // Corollary 2 — DNA integrity (Gemini OB2 + ChatGPT OB1):
     ∀ L > 0: S5(L+1).policy ⊆ S5(L).policy    // child policy is a strict subset of parent
     // Child S5 may be MORE restrictive, never more permissive.
     // NOTE: Corollary 2 is a compound constraint — it requires BOTH A5 AND A2.
     //   A5 establishes the ceiling ordering; A2 establishes the resource containment.
     //   Together they imply policy containment. Neither alone is sufficient.
     //   Formal: A5 ⊕ A2 ⊢ S5(L+1).policy ⊆ S5(L).policy
     rule(S5(L), guard=generate(S5(L+1))):
       enforce: S5(L+1).policy ⊆ S5(L).policy
     // Human: no operational unit can exceed the autonomy declared by S5.
     // The root S5 (human-governed at L=0) sets the maximum ceiling for the
     // entire tree. Sub-level S5s may restrict further but never exceed parent.
     // Single-loop corrections (fix within rules): automatable.
     // Double-loop corrections (change the rules): ALWAYS require human approval.
 A6 — Ashby's requisite variety
     Var(E(L)) ≤ Var(S3(L)) ⊕ Var(S2(L))
     // Static balance: regulatory variety must match environmental variety at every tick.
     // Note (Q-vsf-15, v2.0): this is a snapshot constraint — does not model how
     // Var(S3 ⊕ S2) is maintained or grown. absorption_rate(L) serves as proxy for v1.0.
     // Emergency algedonic bypass (Gemini M1):
     rule(S3*.emergency, guard=V.status = compromised):
       broadcast_revoke(all_active_psi)           // immediate — bypasses tick latency
       priority: CRITICAL                          // does not wait for next tick(L)
       emit φ↑(urgency=CRITICAL, kind="security_breach")
     // Standard S3* audit is periodic (sporadic). Compromise detection must be immediate.
     // This is the algedonic channel operating at maximum urgency — Beer §8.
     // Human: the system can only regulate disturbances it has enough
     // variety to match. If the environment exceeds the regulator, the system
     // cannot maintain viability and must escalate (A7).
     // Persistent violation of A6 is a meta_shift indicator (D12).
 A7 — Escalation axiom
     signal s escalates from L+1 to L  iff:
       resolve(s, V(L+1)) = ∅  ∧  urgency(s) > τ_{L+1}
     // Human: a signal escalates when the current level cannot resolve it
     // AND it exceeds the local threshold. Both conditions required.
     // Resolvable but urgent → stays local.
     // Unresolvable but not urgent → deferred (D10), not escalated.
 A8 — Absorption invariant
     |S(L)| ≤ |S(L+1)| · (1 − r_L)    r_L ∈ (0,1)
     // Human: signal volume decreases exponentially moving up levels.
     // With r=0.9, grandparent sees ~1% of grandchild signals.
     // This keeps the root-level opsroom actionable at any system scale.
 A9 — Budget exhaustion response
     B_exhausted ⟹ (degrade ∨ abort)
     // B_exhausted ⟺ (B_tokens = 0 ∨ B_compute = 0 ∨ B_time = 0)
     // The system must not simply halt — it must choose a governed transition.
     A9b: B_exhausted ⟹ minimal_governance_mode
     minimal_governance_mode:
       emit(events_min)      // φ↑ stays alive — governance survives exhaustion
       skip(S1)              // halt operations
       restrict(S4)          // no environmental adaptation
     A9c: minimal_governance_mode ⊂ degrade
     degrade:
       includes(minimal_governance_mode)
       reduce(capacity)
       preserve(governance)    // governed(V) = True throughout
     A9d: abort ⟺ (critical_failure ∨ policy_violation)
     critical_failure ∈ { system_break, trust_collapse, invariant_violation }
     // A9d is biconditional:
     //   ¬critical_failure ∧ ¬policy_violation ⟹ ¬abort   (no spurious abort)
     //   critical_failure ∨ policy_violation   ⟹  abort    (no silent failure)
     // Degrade is the path for B_exhausted without a critical condition.
     // Abort is only valid when the system cannot safely continue.
 A10 — Escalation target
     escalate(s) → target:
       urgency(s) = LOW      → S3    // local regulation absorbs
       urgency(s) ≥ MEDIUM   → S5    // policy level — may require human (D4)
     // Deterministic: urgency level fully determines target.
     // No escalation without a condition — abort_requires_condition = True.
     // Combined with A7: escalation is triggered by resolve(s)=∅ ∧ urgency > τ_L,
     // and A10 specifies WHERE the escalated signal goes.
 A11 — S4 activation gate
     S4_active ⟺ drift(V, t) > ε
     ε ∈ S5.policy    // drift tolerance threshold — set by governance
     ε ≥ noise_floor  // prevents S4 from activating on measurement noise
     // Biconditional: S4 activates iff drift exceeds threshold.
     // S4 is silent when system is stable — no unnecessary adaptation.
     // Corollary: S4_active = False ⟹ stability(V) ≥ baseline
     // S4 updates AFTER S1 executes in tick(L) — reflects what actually happened.
 ---
 Part III — Definitions
 Definitions are derived from axioms. They can be verified.
 D1 — Viable System
     V(id, L) ::= ⟨ S1, S2, S3, S4, S5, E ⟩ @ level=L
     // Human: a viable system is identified by name and level.
     // All six components are always present — if any is missing, the system
     // must declare itself non-viable until resolved.
     // id is the stable identifier. L is the current frame position.
 D2 — Upward channel
     φ↑ : V_i.id → S3(parent_of(V_i)).id
     φ↑.payload = telemetry/{system_id}/events.jsonl
     φ↑.schema  = connection_schema.json @ "upward"
     // Human: every child writes to events.jsonl keyed by its system_id.
     // system_id is stable across reframe() operations.
 D3 — Downward channel
     φ↓ : S3(parent_of(V_i)).id → V_i.id
     φ↓.payload = telemetry/{system_id}/params.json
     φ↓.schema  = connection_schema.json @ "downward"
     // Human: parent S3 writes params.json per child.
     // Child reads at startup. No other configuration source is valid.
 D4 — Learning loop taxonomy
     single-loop: corrections within pre-authorized rules   → automatable
     double-loop: modifying the pre-authorization rules     → requires_human
     // Pre-authorization pattern:
     // S5 defines classes of structural change once (human confirm).
     // Execution within that class is then single-loop — autonomous.
     // Changing the authorization criteria is always double-loop.
     // Example:
     // S5 sets: policy.allows_promotion = { criteria: controllable(x) ∧ needs_regulation(x) }
     // Once set, S4 may execute promotion autonomously — single-loop.
     // Changing the criteria requires human confirm — double-loop.
     // Budget allocation (S3 bargain):
     B_i = B(parent) · w_i / Σ_j w_j    where w_i = priority_weight(V_i)
 D5 — Recursion condition
     is_viable(u) = True   iff  complexity(u) > τ_viability
     is_viable(u) = False       // u is a leaf — execute_atomic(u)
     // τ_viability is set by the parent S3 in params.json.
 D6 — LLM constraint
     S4(L).uses_LLM = True   only if L ≤ L_max
     L_max = 2               // current system default
     // L0 (tool execution) and L1 (scheduling) are pure Python — no LLM.
     // LLMs have high variety; using them at L0 violates A6 for that level.
 D7 — Opsroom mapping
     opsroom(V(id, L)) = {
       view["system_health"]    ≡ S3(L).audit,
       view["pipeline_status"]  ≡ S1(L).operations,
       view["signal_queue"]     ≡ S2(L).unresolved,
       view["priority_ranking"] ≡ S4(L).environment,
       view["coverage_gaps"]    ≡ S3(L) → S4(L)
     }
     // The opsroom IS the VSM made visible. Five views map 1:1 to five subsystems.
     // Only E(L) varies between domains. The opsroom is domain-agnostic.
 D8 — Signal priority and status
     urgency(s) ∈ { CRITICAL, HIGH, MEDIUM, LOW }
     s.status   ∈ { pending, resolved, undecidable, deferred }
     // urgency is derived — not primitive:
     urgency(s) = f( impact_budget(s), impact_stability(s), model_drift(s) )
     CRITICAL : resolve(s,V)=∅ ∧ urgency=CRITICAL  ⟹  escalate immediately
     HIGH     : present to operator before next tick
     MEDIUM   : log + inform, resolve within session
     LOW      : absorb locally, log only
     // Only CRITICAL and HIGH appear in the opsroom Signal Queue (implements A8).
 D9 — Calibration score
     CalibScore(V, t) = (T_expected(V) / T_actual(V,t)) · (B_used / B_allocated)
     CalibScore ≈ 1.0  →  system behaves as modeled
     CalibScore < 0.7  →  bottleneck — inspect S3 telemetry
     CalibScore > 1.3  →  model pessimistic — recalibrate T_expected
 D10 — Incompleteness state
     resolve(s, V) = ∅  ⟹  s.status = undecidable
     rule(S3(L), guard=s.status=undecidable):
       if urgency(s) > τ_L:
         s ⟹ φ↑(s)              // A7: escalate
       else:
         s.status = deferred     // accumulate — do not escalate yet
     // Deferred pool as meta_shift indicator:
     // |deferred(V(L))| / |signals_total| > μ  ∧  CalibScore(V,t) < 0.7
     //   ⟹  current level structurally insufficient → candidate for reframe()
     // Human: incompleteness is not failure (⊥). It is a structural signal.
     // The pool of deferred signals IS the diagnostic for level adequacy.
 D11 — Structural coupling (ψ)
     ψ(V_A, V_B) : S4_A ↔ S4_B    // horizontal, inter-VSM
     constraints:
       ¬sibling(V_A, V_B)               // A4 takes precedence within same tree
       governed(V_A) ∧ governed(V_B)    // see D11b
       trust_level(V_A, V_B) ≥ limited  // see D11b
       S5(V_A).approve ∧ S5(V_B).approve
     mediated_by: expansion_module ∘ handshake ∘ S3.filter
     // ψ lifecycle states (P4 — ChatGPT):
     ψ.state ∈ { init, validate, negotiate, establish, active, degraded, revoked, abort }
     transitions:
       init      → validate  : guard = governed(requester)
       init      → abort     : guard = ¬governed(requester)
       validate  → negotiate : guard = trust_level ≥ limited
       validate  → abort     : guard = trust_level < limited
       negotiate → establish : guard = human_approved ∧ ψ_rate_limit_ok ∧ no_circular_trust
       negotiate → abort     : guard = effective_scope = ∅
       establish → active    : guard = channel_committed(psi_channels.jsonl)
       active    → degraded  : guard = trust_score ∈ [τ_min, 0.5)
       active    → revoked   : guard = trust_score < τ_min ∨ status_override
       degraded  → active    : guard = trust_score ≥ 0.5   // recovery path
       degraded  → revoked   : guard = trust_score < τ_min
       any       → abort     : guard = timeout ∨ replay_detected
     invariant: ψ.state ∈ states ∧ exactly_one(ψ.state)
     // Replay protection (P5):
     handshake.security:
       nonce_registry: { store: telemetry/{system}/nonces.jsonl, TTL: δ }
       rule(S3, guard=replay_detected): abort ∘ log(S2.access_log)
       replay_detected iff: nonce_seen_before ∧ |t_now - t_nonce| ≤ δ
       δ ∈ [30s, 120s]    // function of network_latency + sync_error; persistent store
     // Handshake — four phases (implemented in src/handshake.py):
     // 1. Identity (pre-trust):   V_A → V_B: { id, schema_version, governed_proof }
     // 2. Trust evaluation:       S3*(V_B): trust_score(V_A) — S3* owned, not S4
     // 3. Credential negotiation: scope = min(offered_scope, trust_ceiling(trust_level))
     // 4. Establish:              D13 checks → human_approved → commit ψ_channel
     // Human: ψ connects S4s because S4 models the environment.
     // Inter-VSM coupling is an exchange of environment models.
     // Two systems sharing S4 models can coordinate without hierarchy.
 D11b — Trust and participation
     governed(x) = has(params(x)) ∧ emits(events(x))
     trust_level  ∈ { none → forbid_ψ, limited → ψ_restricted, trusted → ψ_full }
     // trust_score is owned by S3* (audit) — NOT S4
     // Reason: S4 models the environment. S4 evaluating its own drift = conflict of interest.
     // S3* is the independent auditor — it sees what S3 misses.
     // Formula (OB1 + PATCH 11 + vsl_core.v1.2):
     T(V, t) = 0.6 · decay(T(V, t-1), λ) + 0.4 · audit(V, t)
     audit(V, t) = (0.5 · reachability(V)
                 +  0.3 · stability(S3(V))
                 +  0.2 · (1 - drift(V, t)))
                · (1 - error_rate(S2(V)))
     // reachability: can V be reached via φ↑/φ↓? (0 = unreachable, 1 = full)
     // stability:    fraction of ticks without escalation or degradation
     // drift:        model drift from A11 — high drift reduces audit score
     // error_rate(S2): sibling coordination failures — penalizes noisy coordination
     // λ: decay rate ∈ S5.policy — smooths single bad readings, prevents oscillation
     // Weights sum to 1.0 across audit components.
     // missing history → conservative_default (not full trust)
     // Level-local decay (P3 — ChatGPT OB6):
     param(λ, L)   ∈ S5(L).policy    // each level defines its own decay rate
     rule(λ): λ(L) ≥ λ(L+1)         // higher levels decay faster (more volatile environment)
     // Infrastructure (L0): slow decay (λ≈0.02) — stable, rarely changes
     // Cognitive layer (L2): fast decay (λ≈0.15) — environment shifts rapidly
     // S5(L) sets λ(L); constrained by S5(parent): λ(L) ∈ [λ_min, λ_max] from S5(L-1)
     // Credential rotation invariant (P6 — Q-vsf-8 resolved):
     rotate(V.credential):
       phase_1: provision(new_key)
       phase_2: ψ.add_channel(new_key)       // dual-key window: old + new both valid
       phase_3: wait_for_telemetry(new_key)  // governed(V) maintained throughout
       phase_4: revoke(old_key) ∘ ψ.remove_channel(old_key)
     invariant: governed(V) = True during all phases    // ψ continuity preserved
     // @status → trust_score override (OB3 — closes Q-vsf-6):
     rule(S3*(L), guard=V_peer.status ∈ {deprecated, compromised, unhealthy}):
       trust_score(V_peer) = 0
       terminate(ψ(V_local, V_peer)) ∘ log(S2.access_log)
     // Automatic — no human required for trust=0 (it is a single-loop rule consequence)
     // Reinstating ψ after compromised status = double-loop → requires human (D4)
     rule(S3*["trust"], guard=∅):
       compute: trust_score(V)            // S3* owns computation
     rule(S4["trust"], guard=∅):
       propose: trust_adjustment          // S4 observes drift, proposes — does not decide
     rule(S3["trust"], guard=trust_score < τ_trust):
       restrict(ψ) ∘ notify(S5)          // S3 acts on S3*'s computed value
     // S4 integration of external models via ψ (sandbox pattern):
     rule(S4["ψ"], guard=incoming(ψ, V_B)):
       import: V_B.env_model → sandbox(S4)    // never direct write to env_model
     rule(S4["ψ"], guard=trust_level(V_B) ≥ trusted):
       integrate: sandbox → env_model          // only after trust validation
     rule(S4["ψ"], guard=trust_level(V_B) < trusted):
       sandbox_only                            // isolated — no env_model contamination
     // governed(x) = minimum entry condition for any protocol interaction.
     // A system not participating in φ↑/φ↓ is ungovernable — no ψ allowed.
 D12 — Meta-shift indicator
     meta_shift(V(L)) = True  iff:
       (∃ class(S): ∀ s ∈ class(S): resolve(s, V(L)) = ∅)
       ∧ |class(S)| / |S_total| > μ
       ∧ CalibScore(V(L), t) < 0.7  for t > δ
     // Severity classification:
     meta_shift.severity ∈ { low, medium, high }
     rule(S4["meta"], guard=meta_shift):
       classify:
         low    iff  μ < 0.2  ∧  CalibScore > 0.6    // informational — log only
         medium iff  μ < 0.5  ∧  CalibScore > 0.5    // present in opsroom signal_queue
         high   iff  μ ≥ 0.5  ∨  CalibScore ≤ 0.5    // escalate immediately
     rule(S3["meta"], guard=meta_shift.severity = high):
       s ⟹ φ↑(s, urgency=CRITICAL)
     rule(S5["meta"], guard=∅):
       enforce: no_auto_reframe    // meta_shift never triggers reframe() automatically (D4)
     // Human: meta_shift is a persistent structural pattern, not a single escalation.
     // Severity determines how the signal is treated at the opsroom level.
     // reframe() requires human approval regardless of severity — always double-loop.
 D13 — Structural safety constraints
     // S5-enforced bounds on structural growth (all levels):
     rule(S5, guard=∅):
       enforce: max_recursion_depth          // A1 guarantees finite depth via A2,
                                             // but explicit bound prevents policy gaps
       enforce: promotion_budget_limit       // E→V promotion consumes budget (A2+A3)
       enforce: ψ_rate_limit                 // global limit (D11); per-identity limit (P21)
       enforce: no_circular_trust            // no cycle V_A trusts V_B trusts V_A...
       enforce: no_unbounded_growth(V)       // vertical (depth) + horizontal (ψ + units)
     // Variety Overload — E→V promotion guard (OB4 + P7 — adaptive):
     absorption_rate(L) = |S_resolved(L)| / |S_total(L)|    // S3* computes per tick
     rule(S3*(L), guard=tick):
       max_manageable_units(L) = f(absorption_rate(L), B(L))
       // S3* measures observed capacity; S5 sets policy bounds [max_min, max_ceiling]
     rule(S4(L), guard=promote(x, E → V)):
       if count(S1.units) > max_manageable_units(L):
         resolve(promotion) = ∅
         s ⟹ φ↑(urgency=HIGH, kind="Variety_Overload")
     // A6 + A8: S1 growth bounded by observed absorption, not static config.
     // ownership: S3* computes dynamic threshold; S5 constrains max/min policy bounds.
     // Structural inertia (for single-loop structural operations only):
     resistance(V) = system_size(V) ⊕ coupling_degree(V)
     rule(S3["inertia"], guard=single_loop_structural_op):
       require: Δstructure > threshold_inertia(V)
       // prevents micro-oscillations in pre-authorized structural classes
     rule(S5["inertia"], guard=∅):
       tune: threshold_inertia(V)    // policy sets sensitivity
     // Audit trail — all structural events (S3* extended scope):
     rule(S3*["audit"], guard=∅):
       log: ψ_events | reframe_attempts | promotion_events
       log: reframe_rejected | promotion_rejected    // failure history = equal diagnostic value
 ---
 Part IV — Grammar (BNF)
     system      ::= "@vsm" version NL declaration+
     declaration ::= system_def | rule_def | channel_def | memory_def
                   | semantic_def | question_def | reframe_def
     system_def  ::= "V(" id "," "L=" int ")" ["@status=" status] "=" "{" subsystems "}"
     subsystems  ::= subsystem ("," subsystem)*
     subsystem   ::= "S1:" unit_list
                   | "S2:" coord_spec
                   | "S3:" regulate_spec
                   | "S4:" intel_spec
                   | "S5:" policy_spec
                   | "E:"  env_spec
     unit_list   ::= "[" (id | system_def | "unit(" id ")") ("," ...)* "]"
     rule_def    ::= "rule(" subsystem_ref "," "guard=" guard "):" NL body+
     guard       ::= condition | "∅"
     condition   ::= expr "<" expr | expr ">" expr | expr "=" expr
     body        ::= "  " "∀" quantified | "  " "∃" quantified | "  " action
     channel_def ::= "φ" direction "(" source "→" target "):"  NL
                     "  payload=" file_ref NL
                     "  schema="  schema_ref
     direction   ::= "↑" | "↓"
     coupling_def ::= "ψ(" id "," id "):" NL
                      ("  " constraint NL)+
     memory_def  ::= memory_type "(" id "):" NL field+
     memory_type ::= "episodic" | "semantic" | "procedural" | "policy"
     semantic_def ::= "semantic(" string "):" NL field+
     question_def ::= "question(" string "):" NL "  " expr "?"
     reframe_def  ::= "reframe(" id "," "new_parent=" id "):" NL
                      ("  " action NL)+
     status      ::= "planned" | "onboarding" | "iterating" | "testing"
                   | "production" | "deprecated"
     version     ::= int "." int
 ---
 Part IV.b — Execution Semantics
 tick(L) — Regulation cycle
     tick(L):
       1. read φ↓(params)            // D3: receive config from parent S3
       2. apply S2(coordination)      // A4: prevent sibling conflicts
       3. apply S3(regulation)        // D4: budget allocation, D8: signal triage
       4. for each s with resolve(s)=∅:
            if urgency(s) > τ_L:
              s ⟹ φ↑(s)             // A7: escalate
            else:
              s.status = deferred    // D10: accumulate
       5. execute S1(operations)      // A1: each unit may itself tick(L+1)
       6. update S4(env_model)        // A6: Var(E) ≤ Var(S3) ⊕ Var(S2)
       7. emit φ↑(events)             // D2: report to parent
     // S4 updates AFTER S1 executes — the environment model reflects
     // what actually happened, not what was predicted.
     // Reading φ↓ happens BEFORE regulation — parent params inform current tick.
 reframe(V_id, new_parent_id) — Frame shift
     // Phased execution — four steps, human gate before commit:
     reframe.phases = [propose, validate, simulate, commit]
     phase("propose"):
       actor: S4(V_id)
       trigger: meta_shift(V_id) ∨ external_governance_detected
       action: propose(new_parent=new_parent_id) ⟹ φ↑(urgency=HIGH)
     phase("validate"):
       actor: S3(V_id)
       checks:
         governed(new_parent_id)
         ∧ credential-manager.validate(new_parent_id, scope=limited)
         ∧ B(new_parent_id) ≥ B(V_id)          // A2: child cannot exceed parent
         ∧ S5(new_parent_id).ceiling ≤ S5(current_root).ceiling  // A5 corollary
       on_fail: abort ∘ log(reframe_rejected)
     phase("simulate"):
       actor: S3(V_id)
       model:
         Δsignals   = rerouted φ↑ volume to new_parent
         Δbudget    = B(V_id) consumed from B(new_parent_id)
         Δceiling   = new autonomy ceiling (inherits from new_parent)
       output: impact_report → S5(human)
     phase("commit"):
       actor: S5(L=0) → human confirm
       guard: simulated ∧ human_approved
       action: V(V_id).parent = new_parent_id
       action: establish(φ↑(V_id → new_parent_id))
       action: establish(φ↓(new_parent_id → V_id))
       note: "system files are NOT renamed — L is a view parameter only"
     // reframe() is double-loop — human approval is mandatory (D4).
     // Rejected attempts (validate fail or human deny) are logged by S3*.
     // The failure history is as diagnostic as successful reframes.
 ---
 Part V — Translation rules (Human ↔ VSM)
 5.1 From prose to VSM
 Prose pattern	VSM notation
 "always do X before Y"	`rule(S3, guard=∅): X ∘ Y`
 "never do X directly, use Y"	`∀ op: op.X ∘ Y  // direct X prohibited`
 "if condition, then escalate"	`condition ⟹ φ↑(signal, urgency=HIGH)`
 "X depends on Y being done"	`X.prereq = Y.status == done`
 "confirm before destructive action"	`op ∈ S5.ceiling.requires_confirm`
 "priority order: A, B, C"	`S5.priorities = [A, B, C]`
 "use tool X for task Y"	`S1.unit["Y"].tool = X`
 "this unit is stable / experimental"	`V("unit", L=n) @status=production / @status=iterating`
 "X cannot talk to Y directly"	`X ↛ Y  // A4: route via φ↑ → S3 → φ↓`
 "cannot resolve — escalate"	`resolve(s,V)=∅ ∧ urgency>τ ⟹ φ↑(s)`
 "add governing structure above X"	`reframe(X, new_parent=org)`
 "X and Y share environment model"	`ψ(X,Y) : S4_X ↔ S4_Y  // requires D11`
 5.2 From VSM to human
 `@vsm 0.2` → "this is a VSM spec, version 0.2"
 `V("X", L=0)` → "X is the current root of analysis (frame-local)"
 `S1: [a, b, V("c", L=1)]` → "operations are: a, b, and c (c is itself a full system)"
 `φ↑(V_i → S3)` → "child i reports upward to parent coordination"
 `rule(S3, guard=temp<59): launch ∘ scheduler` → "S3: only launch through scheduler when temp<59°C"
 `s ⟹ φ↑` → "signal s escalates to parent"
 `governed(x)` → "x participates in φ↑/φ↓ — has params, emits events"
 `ψ(A, B)` → "A and B exchange environment models — inter-VSM coupling"
 `reframe(X, new_parent=Y)` → "X is now governed by Y — analysis frame shifts"
 5.3 What does NOT compress well
 The following remain in prose, referenced by `@ref`:
 Why explanations (historical context, incident post-mortems)
 Tradeoff narratives (why option A over option B)
 Onboarding context (what the system is for, in natural language)
 ---
 Part VI — Memory encoding
 6.1 Feedback memory (compressed form)
     @vsm 0.2
     rule(S3["my-platform"], guard=temp<threshold_c):
       ∀ op ∈ S1.remote: launch(op) ∘ thermal_scheduler
       violation: SIGTERM @ temp≥threshold_c
       @ref why="feedback_scheduler_rule.prose.md"
 6.2 Project memory (compressed form)
     @vsm 0.2
     semantic("vsf-architecture"):
       version   = "0.2"
       lifecycle = iterating
       decisions = [D1..D12]              // @ref HANDOFF.vsm.md#decisions
       levels    = {L0: tool-conductor, L1: pipeline-scheduler,
                    L2: [cognitive-layer, token-economy], L3: vsf-meta}
       tasks     = [C, E, F, G, H]       // @ref TASKS.vsm
       public    = {license: MIT, lang: EN, status: pre-release}
 6.3 System config (compressed form)
     @vsm 0.2
     V("my-system", L=0) = {
       E:  { host: "local", runtime: "python3.11" },
       S1: { units: [unit-a, unit-b, unit-c] },
       S2: { anti_loop: "5 retries → escalate", strict_monotonicity: True },
       S3: { scheduler: thermal_scheduler, guard: temp<threshold_c,
             confirm: ["irreversible", "shared-state", "external"] },
       S4: { signals: {HIGH: resolve_first, MED: inform_user} },
       S5: { ceiling: { double_loop: requires_human },
             priorities: [mission_A, mission_B, mission_C] }
     }
 ---
 Part VII — Machine interface
 Instructions for any LLM loading a `.vsm` file:
 Read `@vsm` version first — if version unknown, halt and report
 Axioms are constraints — A1-A8 are never overridden by local rules
 Rules are ordered — higher S-number = higher authority (S5 > S3 > S1)
 `@ref` pointers are lazy — load only if needed
 Compression: lossless for rules, lossy for narrative
 When writing VSM: minimize tokens, use symbols, use `@ref` for prose
 When translating VSM: expand symbols per Part V, rules = imperative, axioms = hard constraints
 L is frame-local — do not assume L=0 is absolute root; use V.id for stable references
 governed(x): verify before processing any ψ request
 ---
 Part VIII — Viability synthesis
     idea("semantic_memory_as_VSM"):
       viability  = HIGH
       complexity = LOW
       precedent  = "cognitive systems theory"
       status     = formalized @ docs/recursive_vsm_theory.md#8
     idea("notation_to_executable"):
       viability  = HIGH
       complexity = HIGH
       precedent  = ["π-calculus/Milner:1992", "ActorModel/Hewitt:1973"]
       gap        = "reduction relation V→V' pending — tick(L) defines one cycle"
       status     = in_progress    // tick(L) formalized in Part IV.b
     idea("new_computational_language

This is a significant evolution of the

. Version
0.3
 moves closer to a truly "computational" grammar by formalizing the

.
To help visualize how these axioms and definitions translate into a functioning system (especially under the hardware constraints of a MacBook 2012 / Parrot OS environment), here is a breakdown of the core logic.
1. The Recursive Control Loop
The core of the framework is
tick(L)
. It ensures that the high-level policy (S5) is always connected to the low-level execution (S1) without saturating the connection.
In your specific setup:

 Acts as the

. It monitors the "Physical Resource Axiom" (

).
L=1 (Parrot OS):
 Acts as the
S1 (Operational Unit)
. It executes the heavy lifting but is bounded by the
Axiom A2
 (Monotonicity).

)
